**LeBron Raymone James** ([^57] [*lə-BRON*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key"); born December 30, 1984) is an American professional [basketball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basketball "Basketball") player for the [Los Angeles Lakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Lakers "Los Angeles Lakers") of the [National Basketball Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Basketball_Association "National Basketball Association") (NBA). Nicknamed " **King James** ", he is the [NBA's all-time leading scorer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_NBA_career_scoring_leaders "List of NBA career scoring leaders") and has won four [NBA championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_NBA_champions "List of NBA champions") from 10 [NBA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_Finals "NBA Finals") appearances, including eight consecutive appearances between 2011 and 2018.[^58] He has also won three [Olympic gold medals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basketball_at_the_Summer_Olympics "Basketball at the Summer Olympics") as a member of the [U.S. national team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_men%27s_national_basketball_team "United States men's national basketball team"). James is widely considered to be one of the greatest basketball players of all time.[^59] [^60]

In addition to ranking [fourth in NBA career assists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_NBA_career_assists_leaders "List of NBA career assists leaders") and [sixth in NBA career steals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_NBA_career_steals_leaders "List of NBA career steals leaders"), James holds several individual honors, including four [NBA MVP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_MVP "NBA MVP") awards, four [Finals MVP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_Finals_Most_Valuable_Player "NBA Finals Most Valuable Player") awards, the [Rookie of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_Rookie_of_the_Year "NBA Rookie of the Year") award, three [All-Star Game MVP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_All-Star_Game_Kobe_Bryant_Most_Valuable_Player_Award "NBA All-Star Game Kobe Bryant Most Valuable Player Award") awards, the inaugural [NBA Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_Cup "NBA Cup") MVP, and the [Olympics MVP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIBA_Men%27s_Olympics_Most_Valuable_Player "FIBA Men's Olympics Most Valuable Player") in the [2024 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basketball_at_the_2024_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men%27s_tournament "Basketball at the 2024 Summer Olympics – Men's tournament"). A record 22-time [All-Star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_All-Star "NBA All-Star") and 21-time [All-NBA selection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-NBA_Team "All-NBA Team") (including a record 13 First Team selections), he has also made six [All-Defensive Teams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_All-Defensive_First_Team "NBA All-Defensive First Team"). The [oldest active player in the NBA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_oldest_and_youngest_NBA_players "List of oldest and youngest NBA players"), he holds records for the [most seasons played](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_NBA_seasons_played_leaders "List of NBA seasons played leaders") with 23, the [most games played](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_NBA_career_games_played_leaders "List of NBA career games played leaders"), the [most minutes played](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_NBA_career_minutes_played_leaders "List of NBA career minutes played leaders"), and the most field goals made in league history.

Born and raised in [Akron, Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akron,_Ohio "Akron, Ohio"), James gained national attention at [St. Vincent–St. Mary High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Vincent%E2%80%93St._Mary_High_School "St. Vincent–St. Mary High School") and was heavily touted as a future NBA superstar for his all-around scoring, passing, athleticism and playmaking abilities.[^61] A [prep-to-pro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prep-to-pro "Prep-to-pro"), the [Cleveland Cavaliers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cleveland_Cavaliers "Cleveland Cavaliers") selected James with the [first overall pick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_first_overall_NBA_draft_picks "List of first overall NBA draft picks") of the [2003 NBA draft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_NBA_draft "2003 NBA draft"). He won Rookie of the Year and quickly established himself as one of the league's premier players, leading Cleveland to its first NBA Finals appearance in [2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_NBA_Finals "2007 NBA Finals") and winning the [scoring title](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_National_Basketball_Association_annual_scoring_leaders "List of National Basketball Association annual scoring leaders") in 2008. After winning back-to-back [MVPs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Most_valuable_player "Most valuable player") in 2009 and 2010, he joined the [Miami Heat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Heat "Miami Heat") as a [free agent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_agent_\(NBA\) "Free agent (NBA)") in 2010, a controversial move announced in the television special *[The Decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Decision_\(TV_program\) "The Decision (TV program)")*.[^62]

With the Heat, James won his first two NBA championships in [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_NBA_Finals "2012 NBA Finals") and [2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_NBA_Finals "2013 NBA Finals"), earning MVP and Finals MVP honors both years. After four seasons in Miami, he returned to Cleveland in 2014, leading the Cavaliers to their first-ever championship in [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_NBA_Finals "2016 NBA Finals"), ending the [Cleveland sports curse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cleveland_sports_curse "Cleveland sports curse"). He signed with the Lakers in 2018, winning another title in [2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_NBA_Finals "2020 NBA Finals") and becoming the first player to win Finals MVP with three different teams. Off the court, James has earned further wealth and fame from numerous endorsement contracts. He is the first player in NBA history to accumulate $1 billion in earnings as an active player.[^63] He was among *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\) "Time (magazine)")* 's [100 most influential people in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100 "Time 100") in 2005, 2013, 2017, and 2019—the most selections for a professional athlete.

## Early life

LeBron Raymone James was born on December 30, 1984,[^1] at [Cleveland Clinic Akron General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cleveland_Clinic_Akron_General "Cleveland Clinic Akron General") [^2] in [Akron, Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akron,_Ohio "Akron, Ohio"),[^65] to 16-year-old Gloria Marie James.[^66]<sup><span title="Page / location: 22">: 22</span> </sup> His father, Anthony McClelland, has an extensive criminal record that includes arson and theft, and was not involved in James' life.[^3] When James was growing up, life was often a struggle for the family, as they moved from apartment to apartment in the more run-down neighborhoods of Akron while Gloria struggled to find steady work.[^71] Realizing that her son would be better off in a more stable family environment, Gloria allowed him to move in with the family of Frank Walker, a local youth football coach who introduced James to basketball when he was nine years old.[^66]<sup><span title="Page / location: 23">: 23</span></sup>

James began playing organized basketball in the fifth grade.[^72] He later played [Amateur Athletic Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amateur_Athletic_Union "Amateur Athletic Union") (AAU) basketball for the Northeast Ohio Shooting Stars.[^71] The team enjoyed success on a local and national level, led by James and his friends Sian Cotton, [Dru Joyce III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dru_Joyce_III "Dru Joyce III"), and Willie McGee.[^66]<sup><span title="Page / location: 24">: 24</span> </sup> The group dubbed themselves the "Fab Four" and promised each other that they would attend high school together.[^66]<sup><span title="Page / location: 27">: 27</span> </sup> In a move that stirred local controversy, they chose to attend [St. Vincent–St. Mary High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Vincent%E2%80%93St._Mary_High_School "St. Vincent–St. Mary High School"), a private [Catholic school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_school "Catholic school") with predominantly white students.[^73]

## High school

### Basketball

As a 6-foot-2-inch (1.88 m) tall freshman, James averaged 18 [points](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Point_\(basketball\) "Point (basketball)") and 6 [rebounds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebound_\(basketball\) "Rebound (basketball)") per game for the St. Vincent–St. Mary [varsity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Varsity_team "Varsity team") basketball team.[^74] The team, known as The Fighting Irish, went 27–0 en route to the Division III state title, making them the only boys high school team in Ohio to finish the season undefeated.[^75] As a sophomore, James averaged 25.2 points and 7.2 rebounds, along with 5.8 [assists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assist_\(basketball\) "Assist (basketball)") and 3.8 [steals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steal_\(basketball\) "Steal (basketball)") per game.[^74] For some home games during the season, St. Vincent–St. Mary played at the [University of Akron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Akron "University of Akron") 's 5,492-seat Rhodes Arena to satisfy ticket demand from alumni and fans, as well as college and NBA scouts who wanted to see James play.[^76] The Fighting Irish finished the season 26–1 and repeated as state champions.[^74] For his outstanding play, James was named [Ohio Mr. Basketball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_Mr._Basketball "Ohio Mr. Basketball") and selected to the [*USA Today* All-USA First Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_Today_All-USA_high_school_basketball_team "USA Today All-USA high school basketball team"), becoming the first sophomore to achieve either.[^74]

In 2001, during the summer before his junior year, James was the subject of a feature article in *[Slam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slam_\(magazine\) "Slam (magazine)")* magazine. Writer Ryan Jones lauded the 16-year-old James, who had grown to 6 feet 7 inches (2.01 m), as "\[possibly\] the best high school basketball player in America right now".[^77] During the season, James also appeared on the cover of *[Sports Illustrated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_Illustrated "Sports Illustrated")*, becoming the first high school basketball underclassman to do so.[^66]<sup><span title="Page / location: 104">: 104</span> </sup> With averages of 29 points, 8.3 rebounds, 5.7 assists, and 3.3 steals per game, he was again named Ohio Mr. Basketball and selected to the *USA Today* All-USA First Team,[^74] and became the first junior to be named male basketball [Gatorade National Player of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gatorade_National_Player_of_the_Year "Gatorade National Player of the Year").[^66]<sup><span title="Page / location: 117">: 117</span> </sup> St. Vincent–St. Mary finished the year with a 23–4 record, ending their season with a loss in the Division II championship game.[^66]<sup><span title="Page / location: 114">: 114</span> </sup> Following the loss, James unsuccessfully petitioned for a change to the NBA's draft eligibility rules in an attempt to enter the [2002 NBA draft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_NBA_draft "2002 NBA draft").[^78] [^79] During this time, he used [marijuana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marijuana "Marijuana"), which he said was to help cope with the stress that resulted from the constant media attention he was receiving.[^80]

During his senior year, James and the Fighting Irish played against several nationally ranked teams, including [Oak Hill Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oak_Hill_Academy_\(Mouth_of_Wilson,_Virginia\) "Oak Hill Academy (Mouth of Wilson, Virginia)") on December 12, 2002. The game was nationally televised on [ESPN2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN2 "ESPN2").[^66]<sup><span title="Page / location: 142">: 142</span> </sup> [Time Warner Cable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_Warner_Cable "Time Warner Cable"), looking to capitalize on James' popularity, offered St. Vincent–St. Mary's games to Ohio-based subscribers for $7.95 per game on a [pay-per-view](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pay-per-view "Pay-per-view") basis throughout the season, but this ended up not being profitable.[^66]<sup><span title="Page / location: 143">: 143</span> </sup> [^81] For the year, James averaged 31.6 points, 9.6 rebounds, 4.6 assists, and 3.4 steals per game,[^74] was named Ohio Mr. Basketball and selected to the *USA Today* All-USA First Team for an unprecedented third consecutive year,[^66]<sup><span title="Page / location: 178">: 178</span> </sup> and was named Gatorade National Player of the Year for the second consecutive year.[^74] He participated in three year-end high school basketball all-star games—the [EA Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EA_Sports "EA Sports") [Roundball Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roundball_Classic "Roundball Classic"), the [Jordan Brand Capital Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Brand_Capital_Classic "Jordan Brand Capital Classic"), and the [McDonald's All-American Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_McDonald%27s_All-American_Boys_Game "2003 McDonald's All-American Boys Game") —losing his [National Collegiate Athletic Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Collegiate_Athletic_Association "National Collegiate Athletic Association") (NCAA) eligibility and making it official that he would enter the [2003 NBA draft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_NBA_draft "2003 NBA draft").[^82]

James was at the center of several controversies during his senior year. For his 18th birthday, James skirted state amateur bylaws by accepting a [Hummer H2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hummer_H2 "Hummer H2") as a gift from his mother, who had secured a loan for the vehicle by utilizing James' future earning power as an NBA player.[^83] This prompted an investigation by the [Ohio High School Athletic Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_High_School_Athletic_Association "Ohio High School Athletic Association") (OHSAA) because its guidelines stated that no amateur may accept any gift valued over $100 as a reward for athletic performance. James was cleared of any wrongdoing because he had accepted the vehicle from a family member and not from an agent or any outside source.[^82] Later in the season, James accepted two throwback jerseys worth $845 from an urban clothing store in exchange for posing for pictures, violating OHSAA rules and resulting in his being stripped of his high school sports eligibility.[^82] James appealed the ruling and his penalty was eventually dropped to a two-game suspension, allowing him to play the remainder of the year. The Irish were also forced to forfeit one of their wins, their only official loss that season.[^84] In his first game back after the suspension, James scored a career-high 52 points.[^85] St. Vincent–St. Mary won the Division II championship, marking their third division title in four years.[^86]

### Football

As an underclassman, James played [wide receiver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wide_receiver "Wide receiver") for St. Vincent–St. Mary's football team.[^66]<sup><span title="Page / location: 51">: 51</span> </sup> He was recruited by some [Division I programs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_NCAA_Division_I_FBS_football_programs "List of NCAA Division I FBS football programs"), including [Notre Dame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Notre_Dame_Fighting_Irish_football "Notre Dame Fighting Irish football").[^87] At the end of his second year, James was named first team all-state, and as a junior, he helped lead the Fighting Irish to the state semifinals.[^88] James did not play during his senior year because of a wrist injury that he sustained in an AAU basketball game.[^89] Some sports analysts, football critics, high school coaches, as well as former and current professional players have speculated that James could have played in the [National Football League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Football_League "National Football League") (NFL).[^4]

## Professional career

### Cleveland Cavaliers (2003–2010)

#### 2003–2004: Rookie of the Year

The [Cleveland Cavaliers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cleveland_Cavaliers "Cleveland Cavaliers") selected James as the first overall pick of the [2003 NBA draft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_NBA_draft "2003 NBA draft").[^94] James chose jersey number 23 in honor of [Michael Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jordan "Michael Jordan").[^95] In his first regular season game, James scored 25 points in a 106–92 loss to the [Sacramento Kings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003%E2%80%9304_Sacramento_Kings_season "2003–04 Sacramento Kings season"), setting an NBA record for the most points scored by a [prep-to-pro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_high_school_draftees "NBA high school draftees") player in his debut performance.[^96] At the conclusion of [the 2003–04 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003-04_NBA_season "2003-04 NBA season"), James became the first Cavalier to receive the [NBA Rookie of the Year Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_Rookie_of_the_Year_Award "NBA Rookie of the Year Award").[^97] and only the third player in league history to average at least 20 points, five rebounds, and five assists per game as a rookie.[^98]

#### 2004–2008: Rise to superstardom

In the [2004–05 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004%E2%80%9305_NBA_season "2004–05 NBA season"), James earned his first [NBA All-Star Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_NBA_All-Star_Game "2005 NBA All-Star Game") selection, contributing 13 points, 8 rebounds, and 6 assists in a winning effort for the [Eastern Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Conference_\(NBA\) "Eastern Conference (NBA)").[^99] Teams took note of his rapid development, and [Denver Nuggets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004-05_Denver_Nuggets_season "2004-05 Denver Nuggets season") coach [George Karl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Karl "George Karl") told *Sports Illustrated*: "It's weird talking about a 20-year-old kid being a great player, but he is a great player... He's the exception to almost every rule." [^100] On March 20, James scored 56 points against the [Toronto Raptors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004-05_Toronto_Raptors_season "2004-05 Toronto Raptors season"), setting Cleveland's new single-game points record.[^101] At the end of the season, James was named to his first [All-NBA Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-NBA_Team "All-NBA Team").[^88] [^101] The Cavaliers again failed to make the playoffs, finishing the season 42–40.[^102] During the 2006 offseason, James signed a three-year, $60 million contract extension with the Cavaliers, with the option for a fourth year.[^103]

At the [2006 All-Star Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_NBA_All-Star_Game "2006 NBA All-Star Game"), James led the Eastern Conference to victory with 29 points and was named the [NBA All-Star Game Most Valuable Player](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_All-Star_Game_Most_Valuable_Player_Award "NBA All-Star Game Most Valuable Player Award") (MVP).[^104] He finished second in overall [NBA MVP Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_MVP_Award "NBA MVP Award") voting, behind [Steve Nash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Nash "Steve Nash").[^105] Under James' leadership, the Cavaliers qualified for [the playoffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_NBA_playoffs "2006 NBA playoffs") for the first time since 1998.[^106] In his postseason debut, James recorded a [triple-double](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple-double "Triple-double") in a win over the [Washington Wizards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005%E2%80%9306_Washington_Wizards_season "2005–06 Washington Wizards season").[^107] In Game 3, he made the first game-winning basket of his career, and another in Game 5.[^108] Cleveland defeated the Wizards before losing to the [Detroit Pistons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005%E2%80%9306_Detroit_Pistons_season "2005–06 Detroit Pistons season") in the second round.[^109]

![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/LeBron_James%27_pregame_ritual.jpg/250px-LeBron_James%27_pregame_ritual.jpg)

James engages in a pre-game ritual of tossing crushed chalk into the air in March 2008. 110 111

The Cavaliers finished the [2006–07 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006%E2%80%9307_NBA_season "2006–07 NBA season") with 50 wins and entered [the playoffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_NBA_playoffs "2007 NBA playoffs") as the second seed in the Eastern Conference.[^112] In the first two rounds, James led the team to wins over the Wizards and [New Jersey Nets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Nets "New Jersey Nets"), earning them a matchup with the Pistons in the Eastern Conference finals. In Game 5, James logged 48 points with nine rebounds and seven assists in a 109–107 double-overtime win. He scored the last 25 points for the Cavaliers, and performed a game-winning [layup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Layup "Layup") with 2.2 seconds left.[^113] In 2012, [ESPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN "ESPN") ranked James' performance as the fourth greatest in modern NBA playoff history.[^114] The Cavaliers won the series in six games, granting them their first [NBA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_NBA_Finals "2007 NBA Finals") appearance, which pitted them against the [San Antonio Spurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006%E2%80%9307_San_Antonio_Spurs_season "2006–07 San Antonio Spurs season").[^115] [^116] The Spurs defeated the Cavaliers in a four-game [sweep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitewash_\(sport\) "Whitewash (sport)").[^117]

On December 11, 2007, James came off the bench for the first time in his career.[^5] He chose to enter the game at the same time as his teammate [Anderson Varejão](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anderson_Varej%C3%A3o "Anderson Varejão") —who had been involved in a pay dispute with Cavaliers management—to prevent fans from booing Varejão.[^118] In February of the [2007–08 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007%E2%80%9308_NBA_season "2007–08 NBA season"), James was named All-Star Game MVP for the second time after a 27-point, eight-rebound, and nine-assist performance.[^119] On March 21, he surpassed [Brad Daugherty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brad_Daugherty_\(basketball\) "Brad Daugherty (basketball)") as the Cavaliers' all-time leading scorer.[^120] James' 30 points per game were the highest in the league, marking his first scoring title.[^121] [^122] Seeded fourth in the East entering [the playoffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_NBA_playoffs "2008 NBA playoffs"), the Cavaliers defeated the Wizards, before being eliminated by the [Boston Celtics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007-08_Boston_Celtics_season "2007-08 Boston Celtics season") in the next round.[^123] [^124]

#### 2008–2010: MVP seasons

At the end of the [2008–2009 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008%E2%80%932009_NBA_season "2008–2009 NBA season"), James finished second in the voting for the [NBA Defensive Player of the Year Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_Defensive_Player_of_the_Year_Award "NBA Defensive Player of the Year Award"). He made his first [NBA All-Defensive Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_All-Defensive_Team "NBA All-Defensive Team"), with 23 [chase-down blocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chase-down_block "Chase-down block") and a career-high 93 total blocks.[^125] [^126] [^127] With James' play and the acquisition of All-Star guard [Mo Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mo_Williams "Mo Williams"), Cleveland went a franchise record 66–16; James became the first Cavalier to win the MVP Award.[^128] [^129] [John Hollinger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Hollinger "John Hollinger") of [ESPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN "ESPN") described James' season as "arguably the greatest individual season in history".[^130]

In [the 2009 playoffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_NBA_playoffs "2009 NBA playoffs"), Cleveland swept the [Pistons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008%E2%80%9309_Detroit_Pistons_season "2008–09 Detroit Pistons season") and the [Atlanta Hawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008%E2%80%9309_Atlanta_Hawks_season "2008–09 Atlanta Hawks season") to earn a matchup with the [Orlando Magic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008-09_Orlando_Magic_season "2008-09 Orlando Magic season") in the Eastern Conference Finals.[^131] The Cavaliers lost Game 1, despite James scoring 49 points on 66 percent shooting.[^114] In Game 2, James hit a game-winner to tie the series 1–1.[^132] Cleveland lost the series in six games; following the loss, James left the court without shaking hands with his opponents, which was viewed as [unsportsmanlike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unsportsmanlike "Unsportsmanlike") by some.[^133] [^134] [^135] James averaged 35.3 points per game during the playoffs.[^136]

In February of the [2009–10 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009%E2%80%932010_NBA_season "2009–2010 NBA season"), James was forced into a temporary [point guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Point_guard "Point guard") role following injuries to players in the Cavaliers' backcourt.[^137] With James' leadership, Cleveland finished the year with the best record in the league.[^138] Despite only playing 39 minutes per game, James received his second NBA MVP Award.[^139] To open [the playoffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_NBA_playoffs "2010 NBA playoffs"), Cleveland advanced past the [Chicago Bulls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009%E2%80%9310_Chicago_Bulls_season "2009–10 Chicago Bulls season") to earn a matchup with the Celtics in the second round.[^140] James was criticized for not playing well in Game 5, shooting only 20 percent on 14 shots and scoring 15 points.[^141] The Cavaliers suffered their worst loss in franchise history; at the conclusion, James walked off the court to boos from Cleveland's crowd.[^142] The Cavaliers were eliminated from the postseason in Game 6.[^140]

### Miami Heat (2010–2014)

#### The Decision

![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/LeBron_James_18112009_1.jpg/250px-LeBron_James_18112009_1.jpg)

James with the Cavaliers in 2009. 143

James became an unrestricted [free agent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_agent_\(NBA\) "Free agent (NBA)") on July 1, 2010.[^144] He was contacted by several teams, including the Bulls, [Los Angeles Clippers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Clippers "Los Angeles Clippers"), [Miami Heat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Heat "Miami Heat"), [New York Knicks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Knicks "New York Knicks"), [New Jersey Nets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn_Nets "Brooklyn Nets"), and Cavaliers.[^145] On July 8, James announced on a live ESPN special titled *The Decision* that he would sign with the Heat.[^146] The special was broadcast from the [Boys & Girls Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boys_%26_Girls_Clubs_of_America "Boys & Girls Clubs of America") of [Greenwich, Connecticut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenwich,_Connecticut "Greenwich, Connecticut") and raised $2.5 million for the organization. An additional $3.5 million was raised from advertising revenue, and was donated to other charities.[^147] The day before the special aired, fellow free agents [Chris Bosh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Bosh "Chris Bosh") and [Dwyane Wade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dwyane_Wade "Dwyane Wade") announced they would sign with Miami.[^148] [^149] James joined with Bosh and Wade in part so he could shoulder less of the offensive load, and he thought he would have a better chance of winning an [NBA championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_championship "NBA championship") with the Heat.[^150] Heat president [Pat Riley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pat_Riley "Pat Riley") played a major role in convincing James to play on the team with Bosh and Wade.[^151] James would be relieved of the burden of scoring, and thought he could be the first player since [Oscar Robertson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Robertson "Oscar Robertson") to average a triple-double in a season.[^150] James, Bosh and Wade were referred to as an [NBA superteam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_superteam "NBA superteam").[^152]

Upon leaving the Cavaliers, James drew criticism from executives, fans, and current and former players.[^153] *The Decision* was viewed as unnecessary. Many thought the prolonged wait for James' choice was unprofessional, as not even the teams courting him were aware of his decision until moments before the show.[^154] Upon learning that James would not be returning to Cleveland, Cavaliers owner [Dan Gilbert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Gilbert "Dan Gilbert") published a letter to fans in which he denounced James' actions.[^155] Some angry fans of the team recorded videos of themselves burning his jersey.[^156] Former NBA players, including Michael Jordan and [Magic Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magic_Johnson "Magic Johnson"), were critical of James, condemning him for forming a superteam with Bosh and Wade in Miami and not trying to win a championship in Cleveland.[^157] [^158] [^159] Some commentators defended James' decision to join Miami by noting the Cavaliers' inability to build strong support around him. They argued other NBA greats had benefitted from talented teammates.[^160] James drew further criticism in an interview with CNN when he claimed race might have been a factor in the fallout from *The Decision*.[^161] [^162] The phrase "taking my talents to South Beach" became a punch line for critics.[^163] [^164] James later expressed regret over his handling of *The Decision*.[^6]

#### 2010–2011: Year of media and fan scrutiny

![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/LeBronJamesDunkingHeat.jpg/250px-LeBronJamesDunkingHeat.jpg)

James goes in for a slam dunk as a member of the Heat in March 2011.

James signed a 6-year, $110 million contract with the Heat on July 10, 2010.[^7] He changed his jersey number to 6, as the number 23 was retired in Miami for [Michael Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jordan "Michael Jordan").[^172] [^173] The Heat threw a welcome party for the " [Big Three](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Three_\(Miami_Heat\) "Big Three (Miami Heat)") " at the [American Airlines Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Airlines_Arena "American Airlines Arena"), during which James declared that the Heat would win multiple championships.[^8] Outside Miami, the spectacle was badly received, furthering negative perception of James.[^177]

Throughout the [2010–11 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010%E2%80%9311_NBA_season "2010–11 NBA season"), the media and opposing fans treated James as a villain.[^178] The Heat struggled to adjust to these new circumstances, going only 9–8 after 17 games.[^179] James later admitted the negativity made him play with an angrier demeanor.[^178] On December 2, James faced the [Cavaliers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010%E2%80%9311_Cleveland_Cavaliers_season "2010–11 Cleveland Cavaliers season") in Cleveland for the first time since departing.[^180] He scored 38 points and led Miami to victory while being booed every time he touched the ball.[^181] The Heat turned their season around and finished as the East's second seed.[^182]

In the [Eastern Conference Semifinals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_NBA_playoffs#Eastern_Conference_semifinals "2011 NBA playoffs"), the Heat was matched with the [Boston Celtics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010-11_Boston_Celtics_season "2010-11 Boston Celtics season").[^183] In Game 5, James scored Miami's last 10 points to seal a series-clinching win.[^184] After the buzzer, James knelt in an emotional moment, later telling reporters it was an extremely personal victory for him and the team.[^185] The Heat advanced to [the Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_NBA_Finals "2011 NBA Finals"), where they lost to the [Dallas Mavericks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010-11_Dallas_Mavericks_season "2010-11 Dallas Mavericks season").[^186] James' Finals average of 17.8 points per game was a 9-point drop from the regular season, the largest drop-off in league history.[^187] [^188]

#### 2011–2013: Back-to-back championships

The [2011–12 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011%E2%80%932012_NBA_season "2011–2012 NBA season") was delayed by a [lockout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_NBA_lockout "2011 NBA lockout"). During that summer, James worked with [Hakeem Olajuwon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakeem_Olajuwon "Hakeem Olajuwon") to improve his [post up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post_up "Post up") game.[^9] As the start of the season approached, James set a goal for himself to leave behind the "villain role" that he had embraced, and said he was hoping to regain a sense of joy on the court.[^178] Miami began the year with a franchise-best 18–6 record, and James was named MVP for the third time.[^10]

In the second round of [the playoffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_NBA_playoffs "2012 NBA playoffs"), Miami lost Bosh to an abdominal injury and found themselves trailing the [Indiana Pacers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011-12_Indiana_Pacers_season "2011-12 Indiana Pacers season") two games to one.[^193] James responded with a 40-point, 18-rebound, and nine-assist outing in Game 4 to even the series.[^194] To compensate for Bosh's absence, the Heat embraced a [small-ball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_ball_\(basketball\) "Small ball (basketball)") lineup with James at [power forward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_forward_\(basketball\) "Power forward (basketball)"), which they retained after Bosh's return in the conference finals against the [Celtics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011%E2%80%9312_Boston_Celtics_season "2011–12 Boston Celtics season").[^195] [^196] Facing elimination in Game 6, James recorded 45 points and 15 rebounds to lead the Heat to victory in a "career-defining performance".[^197] Miami won Game 7 to advance to [the Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_NBA_Finals "2012 NBA Finals"), earning them a matchup with the [Oklahoma City Thunder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011%E2%80%9312_Oklahoma_City_Thunder_season "2011–12 Oklahoma City Thunder season").[^198] [^199] In Game 5, James registered a [triple-double](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple-double "Triple-double") as the Heat defeated the Thunder for their second-ever championship and James' first.[^200] James was voted [Finals MVP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_Finals_Most_Valuable_Player "NBA Finals Most Valuable Player") with averages of 28.6 points, 10.2 rebounds, and 7.4 assists.[^201] His full postseason run was ranked the second best in modern NBA history.[^202]

During January of the [2012–13 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012%E2%80%9313_NBA_season "2012–13 NBA season"), James became the youngest player in NBA history to score 20,000 career points.[^203] In February, he averaged 29.7 points and 7.8 assists while setting shooting efficiency records.[^204] [^205] The Heat began a 27-game winning streak, which is the [third longest in NBA history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_National_Basketball_Association_longest_winning_streaks "List of National Basketball Association longest winning streaks"), and James' performance was described as a "month for the ages".[^206] [^207] Miami finished with a franchise and league best 66–16 record,[^208] and James was named MVP for the fourth time, falling one vote shy of becoming the first player to win unanimously.[^209]

The Heat won the [Eastern Conference Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_NBA_playoffs#Eastern_Conference_finals "2013 NBA playoffs") against the Pacers, then faced the [Spurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012%E2%80%9313_San_Antonio_Spurs_season "2012–13 San Antonio Spurs season") in [the Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_NBA_Finals "2013 NBA Finals").[^210] James was criticized for his lack of aggressiveness and poor shot selection, as Miami fell behind 2–3.[^11] In Game 6, James recorded his second triple-double of the series, including 16 fourth quarter points, to lead the Heat to a comeback victory.[^213] The Heat proceeded to win Game 7, marking their second consecutive championship.[^214] James was named Finals MVP for the second straight season.[^215]

#### 2013–2014: Final season in Miami

On March 3 of the [2013–14 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013%E2%80%9314_NBA_season "2013–14 NBA season"), James scored a career-high 61 points against the [Charlotte Bobcats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlotte_Bobcats "Charlotte Bobcats").[^216] He also set a record for the most three-point field goals made in a 60-point game. Miami earned their fourth consecutive Finals berth, again facing the Spurs.[^12] In Game 2, James led the Heat to a series-tying victory with 35 points on a 64 percent shooting rate.[^221] San Antonio defeated the Heat in five games, ending Miami's quest for three consecutive championships.[^222]

### Return to Cleveland (2014–2018)

In June 2014, James opted out of his contract with the Heat and became an unrestricted free agent.[^223] He returned to the Cavaliers, signing a two-year, $42 million contract, and switched back to the No. 23 jersey.[^13] In contrast to his departure four years earlier, his announcement to return to Cleveland was well received.[^14]

#### 2014–2016: Ending Cleveland's championship drought

![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/LeBron_James_%2815662939969%29.jpg/250px-LeBron_James_%2815662939969%29.jpg)

James takes a shot in November 2014. During that season, James became the Cavaliers' all-time assists leader. 232 233

In January of the [2014–15 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014%E2%80%932015_NBA_season "2014–2015 NBA season"), James missed two weeks due to knee and back strains, which was the longest stretch of missed games in his career at the time.[^234] The Cavaliers advanced to the [Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_NBA_Finals "2015 NBA Finals") against the [Golden State Warriors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014%E2%80%9315_Golden_State_Warriors_season "2014–15 Golden State Warriors season"), making James the first player since the 1960s to play in five consecutive Finals.[^235] [^236] Cleveland took a 2–1 series lead,[^237] but lost in six games.[^238]

During the 2015 offseason, James declined his $21.5 million [player option](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_salary_cap#Options "NBA salary cap") and re-signed with Cleveland on a two-year, $47 million contract that carried a second year player option.[^239] During the [2015–16 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015%E2%80%9316_NBA_season "2015–16 NBA season"), there was speculation that James played a role in the firing of Cavaliers coach [David Blatt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Blatt "David Blatt").[^240] [^241] Despite this distraction, Cleveland finished with 57 wins and the best record in the East.[^242] The Cavaliers advanced to the [NBA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_NBA_Finals "2016 NBA Finals"), losing only two games en route to a rematch with the Warriors, who were coming off a record-setting 73-win season.[^242] [^243]

To begin the series, Cleveland fell behind 3–1.[^244] James responded by registering 41-point games in Games 5 and 6, leading the Cavaliers to consecutive wins.[^245] In Game 7, he posted a triple-double and made a [clutch block](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Block_\(basketball\) "The Block (basketball)") on the Warriors' [Andre Iguodala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andre_Iguodala "Andre Iguodala") as Cleveland claimed victory, becoming the first NBA team to come back from a 3–1 deficit in the Finals. The win also marked the city of Cleveland's first professional sports title in 52 years.[^246] [^247] James became the third player to record a triple-double in an NBA Finals Game 7, and averaged 29.7 points, 11.3 rebounds, 8.9 assists, 2.3 blocks, and 2.6 steals during the series. He was unanimously selected as the Finals MVP.[^248] [^249] During a White House ceremony honoring the Cavaliers for their victory, President Obama praised James' work ethic, determination, selflessness and his "insistence on always making the right play." [^250]

#### 2016–2018: End of second stint in Cleveland

James opted out of his [player option](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_salary_cap#Options "NBA salary cap") and re-signed with the Cavaliers on a three-year, $100 million contract, in which the final year was a player option.[^251] The [2016–17 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_NBA_season "2016–17 NBA season") was marred by injuries and unexpected losses for the Cavaliers;[^252] James described it as one of the strangest years of his career.[^253] [^254] Cleveland finished the season as the East's second seed, with James averaging 26.4 points and career highs in rebounds (8.6), assists (8.7), and turnovers (4.1) per game.[^136]

In Game 3 of the first round of [the playoffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_NBA_playoffs "2017 NBA playoffs"), James led Cleveland to a comeback victory against the [Pacers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_Indiana_Pacers_season "2016–17 Indiana Pacers season").[^255] In Game 5 of the Eastern Conference Finals against the [Celtics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_Boston_Celtics_season "2016–17 Boston Celtics season"), James scored 35 points and surpassed [Michael Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jordan "Michael Jordan") as the NBA's all-time postseason scoring leader. The Cavaliers advanced to the [NBA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_NBA_Finals "2017 NBA Finals") for the third consecutive year, again facing the [Warriors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016%E2%80%9317_Golden_State_Warriors_season "2016–17 Golden State Warriors season").[^256] In Game 4, James logged a triple-double of 31 points, 10 rebounds, and 11 assists, as Cleveland staved off elimination. With this performance, James passed [Magic Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magic_Johnson "Magic Johnson") for the most NBA Finals triple-doubles in a career.[^257] The Cavaliers lost the Finals in five games, despite James's double-double in game 5.[^258] Averaging 33.6 points, 12.0 rebounds, and 10.0 assists in the series, James became the first player to average a triple-double in the Finals.[^259]

![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/68/LeBron_James_vs._Kyrie_Irving_%2823965056038%29.jpg/250px-LeBron_James_vs._Kyrie_Irving_%2823965056038%29.jpg)

James protects the ball from Kyrie Irving in October 2017. The two were teammates in Cleveland for three seasons. 260

Before the [2017–18 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017%E2%80%932018_NBA_season "2017–2018 NBA season"), the Cavaliers overhauled their roster by trading Irving to the [Celtics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017%E2%80%9318_Boston_Celtics_season "2017–18 Boston Celtics season"), who requested the trade in part because he no longer wanted to play with James.[^260] After a slow start, Cleveland rebounded by winning 18 of 19 games.[^261] In a November 3 game, James scored 57 points, the second-highest point total of his career and a franchise record.[^262] [^263] Later in the season, James won his third All-Star Game MVP Award and set an NBA record of scoring in double digits for 867 straight games.[^15]

In the Eastern Conference finals, the Cavaliers defeated the Celtics, giving James his eighth consecutive NBA Finals appearance and sending Cleveland to their fourth straight [Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_NBA_Finals "2018 NBA Finals") against the [Golden State Warriors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017%E2%80%9318_Golden_State_Warriors_season "2017–18 Golden State Warriors season").[^267] [^268] In Game 1, James scored a playoff career-high 51 points in a 124–114 overtime loss.[^269] Afterwards, he injured his hand punching a locker room wall, which hampered him for the remainder of the series.[^270] The Cavaliers lost in the first Finals sweep since 2007.[^271]

### Los Angeles Lakers (2018–present)

#### 2018–2019: Injury and playoff miss

In June 2018, James opted out of his contract with the Cavaliers and became an unrestricted free agent.[^272] He signed with the [Los Angeles Lakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Lakers "Los Angeles Lakers") on a four-year, $153.5 million contract.[^273] [^274] James' agent [Rich Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rich_Paul "Rich Paul") explained: "In 2010, when he went to Miami, it was about championships. In 2014, when he went back to Cleveland, it was about delivering on a promise. In 2018, it was just about doing what he wants to do." [^275] Reactions among Cleveland fans and coaches were more positive than during James' first departure, although ESPN reported that reactions from players, coaches, executives and agents throughout the NBA were mixed.[^276]

The Lakers hoped James would make them championship contenders.[^277] [^278] The team rounded out their roster with a controversial collection of playmakers and veterans, and started the [2018–19 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_NBA_season "2018–19 NBA season") by recording only two wins in their first seven games.[^279] In November, they began a turnaround, which included James' highest-scoring game of the season.[^280] [^281] James suffered a groin injury after Christmas—the first major injury of his career—which caused him to miss 17 consecutive games. The Lakers fell out of playoff contention, marking the first time James missed the playoffs since 2005.[^16] For the first time in twelve years, he did not make the [All-NBA First Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-NBA_First_Team "All-NBA First Team").[^286]

#### 2019–2020: Fourth NBA championship

During the offseason, the Lakers traded several players to the [New Orleans Pelicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Pelicans "New Orleans Pelicans") for " [big man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_man_\(basketball\) "Big man (basketball)") " [Anthony Davis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Davis "Anthony Davis").[^287] James moved to full-time point guard, and the Lakers opened the [2019–20 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_Los_Angeles_Lakers_season "2019–20 Los Angeles Lakers season") with a 17–2 record, matching the best start in the team's history.[^288] [^289] On January 25, 2020, James achieved third place on the [all-time regular season scoring list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_NBA_career_scoring_leaders "List of NBA career scoring leaders"), surpassing former Lakers star [Kobe Bryant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kobe_Bryant "Kobe Bryant") a day before Bryant died in a [helicopter crash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Calabasas_helicopter_crash "2020 Calabasas helicopter crash").[^290] [^291] On January 31, James delivered an improvised eulogy in honor of Bryant, describing him as a "brother" and expressing a desire to continue Bryant's legacy.[^292] [^293] James ended the regular season—which was [suspended](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suspension_of_the_2019%E2%80%9320_NBA_season "Suspension of the 2019–20 NBA season") due to the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic "COVID-19 pandemic") —as the league leader in assists for the first time in his career, averaging 10.2 assists per game.[^294] He earned a record 16th All-NBA Team selection as part of the First Team, extending his record First Team selections to 13.[^295] [^296]

The Lakers entered [the playoffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_NBA_playoffs "2020 NBA playoffs") as the top seed in the [Western Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Conference_\(NBA\) "Western Conference (NBA)") and advanced to the [Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_NBA_Finals "2020 NBA Finals").[^297] In Game 5 against the [Denver Nuggets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_Denver_Nuggets_season "2019–20 Denver Nuggets season"), James helped clinch the series by scoring 38 points.[^298] The Lakers started strong in the Finals, winning the first two games against James' former team, the [Miami Heat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_Miami_Heat_season "2019–20 Miami Heat season").[^299] In Game 5, James scored 40 points and had 13 rebounds and seven assists.[^300] The Lakers eliminated the Heat in Game 6, earning James his fourth NBA championship. With averages of 29.8 points, 11.8 rebounds, and 8.5 assists per game, James was named the Finals MVP, and became the only player to win the award with three different teams.[^301] [^302]

#### 2020–2021: Back-to-back chase

James signed a two-year, $85.7 million extension that kept him with the Lakers through the 2022–23 season.[^303] During the [2020–21 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%9321_NBA_season "2020–21 NBA season"), James became the first player in NBA history to score 10 points or more in 1,000 consecutive games, and the third player to score 35,000 career points.[^17] He sprained his ankle in late March and missed 20 games, the longest absence of his career.[^18] The season was his 17th consecutive season averaging at least 25 points per game, an NBA record.[^19] In a [play-in tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_NBA_Play-in_Tournament "2021 NBA Play-in Tournament") to determine playoff seeds, the Lakers defeated the [Warriors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%9321_Golden_State_Warriors_season "2020–21 Golden State Warriors season") 103–100, with James posting a triple-double.[^20] In the first round of [the playoffs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_NBA_playoffs "2021 NBA playoffs"), the Lakers lost to the [Phoenix Suns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020%E2%80%9321_Phoenix_Suns_season "2020–21 Phoenix Suns season") in six games, marking the first time James lost in the first round.[^21] James made his 17th consecutive All-NBA Team selection, marking a new record for most selections.[^22]

#### 2021–2022: First in the 10K-10K-10K club

For the [2021–22 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021%E2%80%9322_NBA_season "2021–22 NBA season"), James switched his jersey back to No. 6. He had meant to switch two years earlier—to allow Davis to wear No. 23—but Nike had a surplus of merchandise featuring James with No. 23, and so prevented him from making the switch.[^326] In a game against the [Pistons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021%E2%80%9322_Detroit_Pistons_season "2021–22 Detroit Pistons season") on November 21, James was ejected after getting into a scuffle with [Isaiah Stewart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isaiah_Stewart "Isaiah Stewart"). It was only the second time he was ejected from a game, and he was suspended for one game.[^327] [^328] In early December, James achieved his 100th triple-double,[^23] and in February he surpassed [Kareem Abdul-Jabbar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kareem_Abdul-Jabbar "Kareem Abdul-Jabbar") for the most combined points scored in the regular season and playoffs.[^24] James earned his 18th All-Star selection and was celebrated as a member of the [NBA 75th Anniversary Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_75th_Anniversary_Team "NBA 75th Anniversary Team"). At the [2022 NBA All-Star Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_NBA_All-Star_Game "2022 NBA All-Star Game"), James' team achieved its fifth consecutive All-Star victory.[^25]

![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/LeBron_James_at_the_2022_NBA_All-Star_Game.jpg/250px-LeBron_James_at_the_2022_NBA_All-Star_Game.jpg)

James at the 2022 NBA All-Star Game 136

In March, James recorded two 50-point games, becoming the oldest player to have multiple 50-point games in a season.[^26] He recorded his 10,000th career assist, becoming the only player in NBA history to record at least 10,000 points, 10,000 rebounds, and 10,000 assists.[^357] [^358] James suffered an ankle injury in March,[^27] and the Lakers were eliminated from playoff and play-in contention for the first time since [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_Los_Angeles_Lakers_season "2018–19 Los Angeles Lakers season").[^366] [^367] James was named to the All-NBA Third Team for the second time; it was his 18th consecutive All-NBA Team selection, extending the record for most selections.[^368]

#### 2022–2023: All-time scoring record

In August 2022, James signed a two-year, $97 million contract extension with the Lakers, which had a [player option](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_salary_cap#Options "NBA salary cap") in the second year.[^369] [^370] The contract brought his career earnings to $529 million, an NBA record.[^371] [^372] On December 13, James scored 33 points in a 122–118 overtime loss to the Celtics, surpassing [Wilt Chamberlain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilt_Chamberlain "Wilt Chamberlain") for the second-most 30-point games in NBA history, at 516.[^373] [^374]

During January 2023, James recorded a string of accomplishments. He received his 66th [Player of the Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_Player_of_the_Month_and_Week "NBA Player of the Month and Week") award; became the second player in NBA history to reach 38,000 points; achieved his 100th career game with 40 or more points scored; became the first player with a 40-point game against every NBA team; was named as a starter at the [2023 NBA All-Star Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_NBA_All-Star_Game "2023 NBA All-Star Game"), tying Kareem Abdul-Jabbar's record for the most All-Star selections with 19;[^28] and became the first player in NBA history to post a triple-double in his 20th season.[^29] On February 7, James passed Abdul-Jabbar as the [all-time leading scorer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_NBA_career_scoring_leaders "List of NBA career scoring leaders") in NBA history.[^383] The NBA stopped the game with 10.9 seconds left in the third quarter for an on-court ceremony, during which James gave a speech and received the game ball from Abdul-Jabbar.[^30]

James sustained a foot injury in late February, which caused him to miss 13 games.[^31] On April 2, he posted a triple-double during a 134–109 victory over the [Houston Rockets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houston_Rockets "Houston Rockets"), tying [Jason Kidd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jason_Kidd "Jason Kidd") for fourth place on the all-time triple-doubles list.[^396] James finished the regular season with averages of 28.9 points, 8.3 rebounds and 6.8 assists per game, becoming the oldest player in NBA history to average at least 25 points, five rebounds, and five assists in a season.[^397] In Game 4 of the first-round playoffs against the [Memphis Grizzlies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis_Grizzlies "Memphis Grizzlies"), James had 22 points, a playoff career-high 20 rebounds, and seven assists in a 117–111 overtime victory, marking his first game ever with both 20 points scored and 20 rebounds.[^398] He became the oldest player in league history with at least 20 points and 20 rebounds in a playoff game.[^399] In Game 6 of the Western Conference semifinals, James put up 30 points, nine rebounds, nine assists and two steals, in a 122–101 victory over the [Warriors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_State_Warriors "Golden State Warriors") to lead the Lakers to the Western Conference finals. He also won his 41st playoff series, surpassing [Derek Fisher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derek_Fisher "Derek Fisher") for the most playoff series wins in NBA history.[^400] [^401] In the Western Conference finals, the Lakers were swept by the [Nuggets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver_Nuggets "Denver Nuggets"), who would go on to win the [championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_NBA_Finals "2023 NBA Finals").[^402]

#### 2023–2024: NBA Cup MVP and 40K points

For the [2023–24 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023%E2%80%9324_NBA_season "2023–24 NBA season"), James switched back to jersey No. 23 from No. 6 in honor of [Bill Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Russell "Bill Russell"), who had died the previous year and whose No. 6 jersey had been retired league-wide.[^403] With the retirements of [Udonis Haslem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Udonis_Haslem "Udonis Haslem") and Andre Iguodala, James became the NBA's [oldest active player](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_oldest_and_youngest_NBA_players "List of oldest and youngest NBA players").[^404] On November 15, James logged 28 points, 10 rebounds and 11 assists in a 125–110 loss to the [Sacramento Kings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacramento_Kings "Sacramento Kings"), becoming the second-oldest player to record a triple-double.[^405] In November, James became the first player in NBA history to reach 5,000 career turnovers.[^406] On November 27, in a 138–94 loss to the Philadelphia 76ers, James surpassed [Kareem Abdul-Jabbar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kareem_Abdul-Jabbar "Kareem Abdul-Jabbar") to become the player with the most minutes played in NBA games during the regular season and playoffs.[^407] The 44-point loss was the worst of James' career.[^408] On December 9, James and the Lakers won the [inaugural NBA In-Season Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_NBA_In-Season_Tournament "2023 NBA In-Season Tournament"), with James receiving the Tournament MVP award and being selected to the All-Tournament team.[^409]

On January 25, 2024, James was named an All-Star starter for the [2024 NBA All-Star Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_NBA_All-Star_Game "2024 NBA All-Star Game"), marking his 20th NBA All-Star selection, surpassing Abdul-Jabbar for the most All-Star selections.[^410] On February 28, James led the Lakers to a 116–112 comeback win against the [Clippers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clippers_\(NBA_Team\) "Clippers (NBA Team)"). Trailing by 21 points entering the fourth quarter, he outscored the Clippers by himself 19–16 in the final quarter, going 5-for-8 from three-point range. It was the largest fourth-quarter comeback of James' career.[^411] [^412] Three days later, James became the first player in NBA history to reach 40,000 career points.[^413] On March 16, he scored a season-high 40 points in a loss to the Warriors.[^414] On March 31, he put up 40 points on a career-high nine 3-pointers in a 116–104 victory over the [Brooklyn Nets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn_Nets "Brooklyn Nets").[^415] On April 2, James passed [Oscar Schmidt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Schmidt "Oscar Schmidt") to become [the world's all-time scoring leader in basketball history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_basketball_players_with_most_career_points "List of basketball players with most career points").[^416] James finished his 21st season in the NBA averaging 25.7 points, 8.3 assists and 7.3 rebounds a game. He shot 54% from the field and a career-high 41% from 3-point range.[^417]

#### 2024–2025: First father-son duo

On July 6, 2024, James re-signed with the Lakers on a two-year, $104 million contract which carried a [no-trade clause](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trade_\(sports\)#No-trade_clause "Trade (sports)") and had a player option in the second year.[^418] James' son [Bronny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronny_James "Bronny James") had been drafted 55th by the Lakers; on October 22, in a game against the [Minnesota Timberwolves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minnesota_Timberwolves "Minnesota Timberwolves"), James and Bronny became the first father-son duo to play in an NBA game together.[^32] On November 13, James logged a triple-double against the [Grizzlies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis_Grizzlies "Memphis Grizzlies"), setting a new record for the oldest player to record a triple-double in three consecutive games.[^423] Two days later, he recorded his fourth consecutive triple-double of the season, breaking his own record for the most consecutive triple-doubles.[^424] On December 1, James became only the second player (after Kareem Abdul-Jabbar) to score 15,000 career field goals, and on December 19 he passed Abdul-Jabbar for the most regular season minutes played in a career.[^425] [^426]

On January 3, 2025, James put up 30 points and eight assists in a 119–102 win over the [Hawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlanta_Hawks "Atlanta Hawks"), surpassing Michael Jordan for the most 30-point regular season games in NBA history.[^427] On February 6, James recorded a season-high 42 points in a win over the Warriors, making James the oldest player to score 40 or more points in a game.[^428] James was selected for his [21st NBA All-Star Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_NBA_All-Star_Game "2025 NBA All-Star Game"), but missed the game due to foot and ankle soreness, which ended his streak of 20 consecutive All-Star game starts.[^429] On March 4, James surpassed 50,000 career points across the regular season and playoffs.[^430] Four days later, he experienced a groin injury, which kept him off the court for seven games.[^431] [^432] On March 26, he scored 13 points and a game-winning [buzzer-beater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buzzer-beating "Buzzer-beating") in a 120–119 victory against the [Pacers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Pacers "Indiana Pacers").[^433]

#### 2025–2026: 23rd NBA season

![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/LeBron_James_%2855095816551%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-LeBron_James_%2855095816551%29_%28cropped%29.jpg)

James with the Los Angeles Lakers in 2026

After missing the Lakers' first 14 games due to [sciatica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sciatica "Sciatica"), James made his season debut on November 18 in a 140–126 win over the [Utah Jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utah_Jazz "Utah Jazz"). He became the first player to play in 23 NBA seasons.[^434] On December 4, in a 123–120 win over the [Toronto Raptors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toronto_Raptors "Toronto Raptors"), James assisted on [Rui Hachimura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rui_Hachimura "Rui Hachimura") 's game-winning, buzzer-beating 3-pointer, finishing the game with eight points.[^435] [^436] This ended his record-making streak of 1,297 consecutive games with at least 10 points.[^437] James passed Kobe Bryant to become the player with the most double-doubles with 30+ points in Lakers franchise history.[^438] After sitting out a game against the [San Antonio Spurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Antonio_Spurs "San Antonio Spurs") on February 10, James became ineligible from receiving any regular season awards by missing his 18th game of the season. As a result, his record streak of 21 consecutive [All-NBA Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-NBA_Team "All-NBA Team") selections came to an end.[^439] On February 12, he became the oldest player in NBA history to post a triple-double, recording 28 points, 12 assists, and 10 rebounds in a victory over the [Dallas Mavericks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025%E2%80%9326_Dallas_Mavericks_season "2025–26 Dallas Mavericks season").[^440] On February 28, James reached 1,000 three-pointers made as a Laker.[^441] [^442] On March 5, in a game against the [Denver Nuggets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver_Nuggets "Denver Nuggets"), James made his 15,838th career [field goal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_goal_\(basketball\) "Field goal (basketball)") and surpassed [Kareem Abdul-Jabbar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kareem_Abdul-Jabbar "Kareem Abdul-Jabbar") for the most career field goals made in NBA history.[^443] [^444] On March 21, James played his 1,612th career game, passing [Robert Parish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Parish "Robert Parish") to become the [all-time leader in career games played](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_NBA_career_games_played_leaders "List of NBA career games played leaders").[^445] On March 30, James (1229 wins) surpassed Jabbar (1228 wins) career wins (regular season and playoffs) for the most wins in NBA history.

## National team career

![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/41/LeBron_James_vs_Yao_Ming_-_Olympics_2008.jpg/250px-LeBron_James_vs_Yao_Ming_-_Olympics_2008.jpg)

James attempting a shot over China's Yao Ming at the 2008 Summer Olympics

As a 19-year-old rookie, James made his debut for the [United States national team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_men%27s_national_basketball_team "United States men's national basketball team") at the [2004 Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2004_United_States_men%27s_Olympic_basketball_team "2004 United States men's Olympic basketball team") in Athens, Greece, although he spent most of the event on the bench.[^446] [^447] Team USA finished with a bronze medal, becoming the first U.S. basketball team to return home without a gold medal since the NBA started allowing active players to participate in the Olympics.[^448] James felt he was not given "a fair opportunity to play".[^447]

At the [2006 FIBA World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_FIBA_World_Championship "2006 FIBA World Championship") in Japan, James took on a greater role for Team USA, averaging 13.9 points, 4.8 rebounds, and 4.1 assists per game as co-captain.[^449] [^450] The team finished the tournament with an 8–1 record, winning another bronze medal.[^449] During this time, James was known for his disrespectful attitude toward the national team's staff, coaches and players. While assembling the [2008 Olympic squad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_United_States_men%27s_Olympic_basketball_team "2008 United States men's Olympic basketball team"), Team USA managing director [Jerry Colangelo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerry_Colangelo "Jerry Colangelo") and coach [Mike Krzyzewski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Krzyzewski "Mike Krzyzewski") told James he could only play if he improved his attitude, and James complied. Colangelo said that James matured significantly as both a player and a person as a result of the ultimatum.[^33]

At the [FIBA Americas Championship 2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIBA_Americas_Championship_2007 "FIBA Americas Championship 2007"), James put up 31 points against Argentina in the championship game, the most ever by an American in an Olympic qualifier.[^454] Team USA went 10–0, winning the championship and qualifying for the [2008 Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Olympics "2008 Olympics") in [Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beijing "Beijing"),[^446] where they went undefeated and won the gold medal.[^455] [^456]

![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b0/Mansoor_Ahmed_photos_of_Team_USA_basketball_at_London_2012_Olympics.jpg/250px-Mansoor_Ahmed_photos_of_Team_USA_basketball_at_London_2012_Olympics.jpg)

James attempting a dunk at the 2012 Summer Olympics

James did not play at the [2010 FIBA World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_States_men%27s_FIBA_World_Championship_team "2010 United States men's FIBA World Championship team") but became the leader of Team USA ahead of the [2012 Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_United_States_men%27s_Olympic_basketball_team "2012 United States men's Olympic basketball team") in London.[^457] [^458] During a game against [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_men%27s_national_basketball_team "Australia men's national basketball team"), he recorded the first triple-double in U.S. Olympic basketball history with 11 points, 14 rebounds, and 12 assists.[^34] Team USA won the gold medal, again defeating Spain in the final game.[^460] James contributed 19 points in the victory, becoming the all-time leading scorer in U.S. men's basketball history.[^446] [^460] He became the second player, alongside Michael Jordan, to win an NBA MVP award, NBA championship, NBA Finals MVP, and Olympic gold medal in the same year.[^461]

After a 12-year hiatus from the Olympics, James joined the [2024 Olympic squad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_men%27s_Olympic_basketball_team "2024 United States men's Olympic basketball team") in Paris as team captain at 39 years old.[^462] He was one of two Team USA [flag-bearers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard-bearer "Standard-bearer") for the [opening ceremony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Summer_Olympics_opening_ceremony "2024 Summer Olympics opening ceremony") of the games, becoming the first male basketball player to receive the honor.[^463] [^464] James led the team in rebounds and assists, and recorded his second Olympic triple double,[^465] becoming the first player in Olympic history with two triple doubles.[^466] Team USA won their fifth straight gold medal, defeating France 98–87 in the final game.[^467] With averages of 14.2 points, 6.8 rebounds and 8.5 assists in the tournament, James was named the [FIBA Men's Olympics MVP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIBA_Men%27s_Olympics_MVP "FIBA Men's Olympics MVP") and was selected to the [FIBA Men's Olympics All-Star Five](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIBA_Men%27s_Olympics_All-Star_Five "FIBA Men's Olympics All-Star Five").[^468] James became the third male basketball player to win three gold medals at the Olympics.

## Player profile

Standing 6 feet 9 inches (2.06 m) tall and weighing 250 pounds (113 kg),[^469] James has played the majority of his career at the [small forward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_forward "Small forward") and [power forward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_forward "Power forward") positions,[^136] but he has also been deployed at other positions when necessary.[^470] His playing style, which is athletic and versatile,[^190] has drawn comparisons to [Basketball Hall of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basketball_Hall_of_Fame "Basketball Hall of Fame") players [Oscar Robertson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Robertson "Oscar Robertson"), [Magic Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magic_Johnson "Magic Johnson") and [Michael Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jordan "Michael Jordan").[^35]

### Offense

As an 18-year-old rookie, James led the Cavaliers in scoring.[^474] He holds numerous "youngest to" distinctions,[^475] [^476] including being the youngest player to score 30,000 career points.[^477] During his first stint in Cleveland, James was primarily used as an on-ball [point forward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Point_forward "Point forward"), and although his shooting tendencies were perimeter-oriented,[^190] he established himself as one of the best [slashers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slasher_\(basketball\) "Slasher (basketball)") and finishers in basketball.[^478] [^479] His combination of speed and size often created matchup problems for opposing teams because James was capable of charging past larger defenders and overpowering smaller ones.[^480] James was frequently criticized for not having a reliable [jump shot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jump_shot_\(basketball\) "Jump shot (basketball)") or post game. Teams would try to exploit these weaknesses by forcing him to settle for three-pointers and long two-pointers, a strategy famously used by Spurs coach [Gregg Popovich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregg_Popovich "Gregg Popovich") in the 2007 Finals, during which James scored on only 36 percent of his two-point field goal attempts, and on only 20 percent of his three-point attempts.[^481]

![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Lebronred.jpg/250px-Lebronred.jpg)

James drives to the basket in March 2008. A deft finisher, he led the NBA in scoring and shooting percentage at the rim in 2013. 482

In Miami, Heat coach [Erik Spoelstra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erik_Spoelstra "Erik Spoelstra") changed James' role to a more unconventional one.[^190] James spent more time in [the post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Key_\(basketball\) "Key (basketball)") and improved his shot selection and accuracy on jump shots.[^190] [^483] He also learned how to work as an off-ball cutter in the Heat's "pass-happy" offense.[^484] With these improvements, James' overall scoring efficiency rose significantly.[^485] During this time, ESPN's Tom Haberstroh called James' free-throw shooting his biggest weakness.[^486]

In 2015 and 2016, James began to experience subtle age-related declines in productivity,[^487] posting his lowest scoring averages since his rookie season.[^136] [^488] His shooting also temporarily regressed, and he briefly ranked as the NBA's worst high-volume shooter from outside [the key](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Key_\(basketball\) "Key (basketball)").[^489] Despite these changes, he remained an elite offensive player who beat defenses with body control, strength, and varying attack speeds.[^480]

For most of his career, James has controlled the offense as the primary ball handler on his team.[^490] His playmaking ability is generally considered one of his premier skills, and some analysts rank him among the greatest passers in NBA history.[^491] By exploiting his size, vision, and the attention he garners from opposing defenses,[^492] he creates easy points for his teammates with precise assists.[^493] James executes unconventional passes, including after leaving his feet and through defensive traffic.[^494] His uncanny tendency to find an open player has pushed opposing teams to incorporate elements of [zone defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zone_defense "Zone defense") into their strategies.[^480] Early in his career, James was criticized for overpassing in pressure situations, in particular for passing instead of shooting in the waning seconds of close games;[^495] [^496] however, as his career progressed, James' [clutch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clutch_\(sports\) "Clutch (sports)") performance was viewed more favorably.[^497]

### Defense

![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4d/LeBron_James_%2840626830701%29.jpg/250px-LeBron_James_%2840626830701%29.jpg)

James defending against Joe Harris in February 2018

At the beginning of his NBA career, James was considered a poor defensive player,[^498] but he improved steadily over the years. In 2009, he demonstrated proficiency at the [chase-down block](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chase-down_block "Chase-down block").[^126] In Miami, he developed into a more versatile defensive player, and the Heat relied on him to guard all five positions.[^499] Between 2012 and 2016, he was ranked among the top three defenders in the NBA by general managers, and was named the best perimeter defender in the league ahead of the [2014–15 season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014%E2%80%9315_NBA_season "2014–15 NBA season").[^500] Along with [Shane Battier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shane_Battier "Shane Battier") and Dwyane Wade, Miami used James in an ultra-aggressive defensive scheme,[^501] with James [cheating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_basketball_terms#cheat "Glossary of basketball terms") off the ball to help out inside or to get into rebounding position.[^502] Beginning in 2014, some analysts saw a regression in his defensive impact, stemming from a lack of effort and expected age-related declines.[^503] [^504] During his second stint in Cleveland, James' defense progressively declined. After missed drives on offense, he often dawdled back on defense while complaining to the referees; he provided less help [off the ball,](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_basketball_terms#off_the_ball "Glossary of basketball terms") and was less aggressive in [switching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Switch_\(basketball\) "Switch (basketball)").[^505] James himself admitted to taking plays off at times, referring to this approach as "chill mode".[^506] He eventually developed a reputation for raising his defensive impact in the playoffs, which some analysts referred to as "Playoff LeBron".[^507]

## Legacy

![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Lebron_wizards_2017_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-Lebron_wizards_2017_%28cropped%29.jpg)

James with the Cavaliers in 2017. According to NBA analyst Brian Windhorst, "No one has ever had as much hype as James has had to live up to, and James has delivered on every last drop." 508

James left high school as one of the most [hyped](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_circus "Media circus") prospects in NBA history.[^36] Upon entering the league, he made an immediate impact and was voted Rookie of the Year in his debut season.[^513] As of 2024, James has been named to 21 All-NBA Teams, including 13 times to the First Team, which are both NBA records.[^323] His four MVP awards are matched only by Michael Jordan, Kareem Abdul-Jabbar, Wilt Chamberlain, and [Bill Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Russell "Bill Russell"); James and Russell are the only players to win four MVP awards in a five-year span.[^514] James has also won four Finals MVP Awards, which is the second-most all-time,[^515] and earned All-Defensive honors every season from 2009 to 2014.[^136] While James has never won the Defensive Player of the Year Award, he has finished second in the voting twice and lists it among his goals.[^125] [^516] His teams have appeared in the Finals ten times and won four championships; his 10 Finals appearances are tied for third all-time.[^37] Some analysts have criticized him for not having a better Finals record, while others have countered that James usually performed well but his team was defeated by superior competition.[^38]

Based on his career longevity and on-court performances, sports publications have consistently included James in rankings of the best basketball players in history,[^39] and he was named the Associated Press Male Athlete of the Decade for the 2010s.[^528] His high rankings have resulted in frequent debates about whether James or Michael Jordan is the greatest player of all time.[^40] In a 2016 interview with *Sports Illustrated*, James said he was hoping to surpass Jordan as the greatest player.[^529] In February 2018, *[The Ringer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Ringer_\(website\) "The Ringer (website)")* spent an entire week devoted to both players, with [Bill Simmons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Simmons "Bill Simmons") ultimately concluding that Jordan was still ahead.[^530] James has ranked second behind Jordan in polls, the results of which show a strong correlation with the age of respondents, as older voters more often choose Jordan.[^41] *[Business Insider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_Insider "Business Insider")* stated that the data "would suggest that younger, more-engaged NBA fans lean toward James, as he's still playing" while older generations "who watched Jordan play and tune in less today lean toward Jordan." [^534] [^533] Referring to James as the best challenger to Jordan's status as the greatest player, Sam Quinn of CBS Sports stated that "in the rings-obsessed basketball discourse", Jordan having more titles and an "unblemished Finals record holds significant weight".[^529]
