In addition to praising James' on-court accomplishments, analysts have also noted his influence on player empowerment throughout the NBA, which stemmed from his willingness to change teams during free agency. Ben Golliver of *[The Washington Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Washington_Post "The Washington Post")* said that James' move to the Heat in 2010 "defined a decade of player movement", and that he "fundamentally flipped the power balance between stars and their organizations." [^535] Players have also remarked on his influence, such as Warriors forward [Draymond Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Draymond_Green "Draymond Green"), who reflected: "We've taken control of our destiny. And I think a lot of people hate that... I think the doors that he's opened for athletes and especially basketball players is his biggest accomplishment." [^536] Multiple current and former NBA players have suggested James' free agency decisions catalyzed the league-wide trend in " [superteams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superteams_in_the_NBA "Superteams in the NBA") " in the 21st century.[^42]

### Public image

James is often considered by fellow NBA players and the media to be the "face of the NBA", frequently alongside [Golden State Warriors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_State_Warriors "Golden State Warriors") superstar [Stephen Curry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Curry "Stephen Curry").[^542] [^543] In 2014, James reportedly asked NBA commissioner [Adam Silver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Silver "Adam Silver") to increase the duration of the All-Star break, and it was extended the following season.[^544] On February 13, 2015, James was elected the first vice president of the [National Basketball Players Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Basketball_Players_Association "National Basketball Players Association") (NBPA).[^545] Throughout the 2010s, James was usually ranked as the best player in the NBA each season by ESPN and *Sports Illustrated*.[^546] [^547]

Throughout his career, James has been ranked by *Forbes* as one of the world's most influential athletes,[^43] and has been listed by *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\) "Time (magazine)")* as one of the [100 most influential people in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100 "Time 100").[^550] During his first stint with the Cavaliers, James was adored by local fans, and [Sherwin-Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sherwin-Williams "Sherwin-Williams") displayed a giant Nike-produced banner of James at its world headquarters.[^551] Despite their affection for James, some Cleveland fans were irritated when he wore a Yankees cap at a [Cleveland Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cleveland_Indians "Cleveland Indians") baseball game against the [New York Yankees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Yankees "New York Yankees").[^552] Following his actions during the 2010 free agency period and *The Decision*, James was listed as one of the most disliked athletes in the United States.[^553] By 2013, his image had mostly recovered and he was reported by ESPN as the most popular player in the NBA for the second time in his career.[^554] In 2014, James was named the most popular male athlete in America by the Harris Poll.[^555] He has led the league in jersey sales six times.[^556] As of June 2023, James is the [most followed basketball player on Instagram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Most_followed_users_on_Instagram "Most followed users on Instagram"), with over 155 million followers.[^557]

![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/d/d2/Vogue-Cover_James-Bundchen.jpg) ![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/Harry_R._Hopps%2C_Destroy_this_mad_brute_Enlist_-_U.S._Army%2C_03216u_edit.jpg/250px-Harry_R._Hopps%2C_Destroy_this_mad_brute_Enlist_-_U.S._Army%2C_03216u_edit.jpg)

Critics noted similarities between the April 2008 *[Vogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vogue_\(magazine\) "Vogue (magazine)")* magazine cover, which featured James and [Gisele Bündchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gisele_B%C3%BCndchen "Gisele Bündchen"), and images of King Kong.

[Memorabilia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_memorabilia "Sports memorabilia") associated with James is highly sought after; two of James' [rookie cards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rookie_card "Rookie card") are among the most expensive [basketball cards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basketball_card "Basketball card") ever sold at auction, with one selling for $1.8 million in July 2020.[^558] [^559] All jerseys worn in the [2020 NBA All-Star Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_NBA_All-Star_Game "2020 NBA All-Star Game") were auctioned by the NBA and NBPA to raise funds for charity; James' jersey sold for $630,000, setting a record for a modern-day sports jersey.[^560]

In March 2008, James was featured on the cover of *[Vogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vogue_\(magazine\) "Vogue (magazine)")* magazine with [Gisele Bündchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gisele_B%C3%BCndchen "Gisele Bündchen").[^561] ESPN columnist [Jemele Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jemele_Hill "Jemele Hill") considered the cover "distasteful", noting similarities between it and images of [King Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Kong "King Kong") capturing a fair-skinned love interest. Other critics echoed Hill's sentiments and accused the magazine of creating imagery that associates black men with aggressive animals.[^44]

A biography of James, titled *LeBron*, was published on April 11, 2023, by [Jeff Benedict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Benedict "Jeff Benedict"). The book was based on three years of research and more than 250 interviews.[^566] In 2025, [Mattel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mattel "Mattel") announced that it was adding a [Ken doll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ken_\(doll\) "Ken (doll)") modeled after James to its [Barbie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbie_\(media_franchise\) "Barbie (media franchise)") toy line. The doll is an inch taller than a standard Ken doll.[^567] The same year, James was named an honorary co-chair of the [Met Gala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Met_Gala "Met Gala") in [Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan "Manhattan"), but he did not attend due to a knee injury.[^45]

## Media and business interests

### Endorsements

James has signed numerous endorsement contracts, including with [AT&T](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AT%26T "AT&T"),[^571] [Audemars Piguet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audemars_Piguet "Audemars Piguet"),[^572] [Beats by Dre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beats_Electronics "Beats Electronics"),[^573] [Blaze Pizza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blaze_Pizza "Blaze Pizza"),[^574] [Coca-Cola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coca-Cola "Coca-Cola"),[^575] [Dunkin' Brands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunkin%27_Brands "Dunkin' Brands"),[^576] [Intel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intel "Intel"),[^577] [Kia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kia "Kia"),[^578] [McDonald's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonald%27s "McDonald's"),[^579] [Nike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike,_Inc. "Nike, Inc."),[^575] [PepsiCo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PepsiCo "PepsiCo"),[^580] [State Farm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Farm "State Farm"),[^581] and [Taco Bell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taco_Bell "Taco Bell").[^582] Coming out of high school, James was the subject of a three-way bidding war among Nike, [Reebok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reebok "Reebok"), and [Adidas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adidas "Adidas");[^583] he eventually signed a seven-year deal with Nike in May 2003 [^584] [^585] for approximately $90 million.[^586] James' signature Nike shoes have been a successful product.[^587]

In 2011, [Fenway Sports Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fenway_Sports_Group "Fenway Sports Group") became the sole global marketer of James' rights; as part of the deal, James was granted a minority stake in the English [Premier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premier_League "Premier League") soccer club [Liverpool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liverpool_F.C. "Liverpool F.C.").[^588] [^589] That same year, James co-founded the designer retail store UNKNWN in [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami "Miami").[^590] When [Apple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Inc. "Apple Inc.") acquired [Beats Electronics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beats_Electronics "Beats Electronics") in 2014, James earned more than $30 million through the sale of his stake in the company.[^591] James has been listed as one of the [richest celebrities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_celebrities_by_net_worth "List of celebrities by net worth"), and was the first active NBA player to become a billionaire.[^592] [^593] In 2013, he surpassed Bryant as the highest paid basketball player in the world, with earnings of $56.5 million.[^594] In 2015, James was ranked the sixth highest earning athlete,[^595] and in 2016 was ranked the third highest.[^596]

During [Super Bowl LVI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LVI "Super Bowl LVI") in 2022, a [Crypto.com](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crypto.com "Crypto.com") commercial depicted James offering advice to a CGI version of his younger self. The commercial, which was produced by James' [SpringHill Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SpringHill_Company "SpringHill Company"), inspired a series of internet [memes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meme "Meme") about possible advice James could have offered.[^597] Crypto subsequently distributed 5,550 [non-fungible tokens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-fungible_token "Non-fungible token") featuring James to randomly-selected recipients who scanned a [QR code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/QR_code "QR code") in the commercial.[^598] During the same year, the LeBron James Family Foundation partnered with Crypto to educate underserved Akron communities about [blockchains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blockchain "Blockchain") and [Web3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web3 "Web3") tools.[^599]

### Entertainment

James hosted the 33rd-season premiere of *[Saturday Night Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saturday_Night_Live "Saturday Night Live")* in 2013, and co-hosted the 2007 [ESPY Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPY_Awards "ESPY Awards") with [Jimmy Kimmel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Kimmel "Jimmy Kimmel").[^600] [^601] In 2012, James had a cameo role on the [HBO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HBO "HBO") series *[Entourage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entourage_\(American_TV_series\) "Entourage (American TV series)")*, and in 2015 he appeared as himself in the [Judd Apatow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judd_Apatow "Judd Apatow") film *[Trainwreck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trainwreck_\(film\) "Trainwreck (film)")*.[^46] Also in 2015, James' digital video company [Uninterrupted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uninterrupted "Uninterrupted") raised $15.8 million from [Warner Bros. Entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Bros._Entertainment "Warner Bros. Entertainment") and [Turner Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turner_Sports "Turner Sports") to expand its efforts to bring athlete-created content to fans. Uninterrupted is hosted on the website [Bleacher Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bleacher_Report "Bleacher Report") and has been used by athletes such as [Rob Gronkowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rob_Gronkowski "Rob Gronkowski") and [Richard Sherman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Sherman_\(American_football\) "Richard Sherman (American football)").[^605] In 2017, Uninterrupted produced the documentary *[The Carter Effect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Carter_Effect "The Carter Effect")*, which was screened at the [Toronto International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toronto_International_Film_Festival "Toronto International Film Festival").[^606] [^607]

James and his business partner [Maverick Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maverick_Carter "Maverick Carter") own the production company [SpringHill Entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SpringHill_Entertainment "SpringHill Entertainment").[^608] The company's first project was the documentary *[More Than a Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/More_Than_a_Game "More Than a Game")* (2009), which chronicled James' high school years.[^609] Series produced by SpringHill include the game show *[The Wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Wall_\(game_show\) "The Wall (game show)")*,[^610] the sports documentary series *[Becoming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Becoming_\(2020_TV_series\) "Becoming (2020 TV series)")*,[^611] the sitcom *[Survivor's Remorse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Survivor%27s_Remorse "Survivor's Remorse")*,[^612] and the animated web series *[The LeBrons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_LeBrons "The LeBrons")*.[^613] In 2016, [CNBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CNBC "CNBC") aired a [reality series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reality_series "Reality series") hosted by James called *[Cleveland Hustles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cleveland_Hustles "Cleveland Hustles")*, which featured aspiring entrepreneurs competing to open businesses under the mentorship of a Cleveland investor.[^614] [^615] In 2023, SpringHill and Warner Bros. released a reboot of the *[House Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_Party_\(2023_film\) "House Party (2023 film)")* film series, which contains a cameo by James.[^616] [^617]

James was the executive producer of rapper [2 Chainz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2_Chainz "2 Chainz") 's 2019 album *[Rap or Go to the League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rap_or_Go_to_the_League "Rap or Go to the League")*.[^618] A press release said the album celebrates "black excellence" while challenging "the notion that the only way out of the inner city is either to become a rapper or a ball player." [^619] In June 2022, it was announced that James was launching a media company, Hana Kuma, in partnership with the professional tennis player [Naomi Osaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naomi_Osaka "Naomi Osaka") and Maverick Carter.[^620]

In March 2024, James and [JJ Redick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JJ_Redick "JJ Redick") launched a podcast called *[Mind the Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mind_the_Game "Mind the Game")*, where the two have "pure conversations about basketball." [^47] The podcast was suspended after Redick was hired as head coach for the Los Angeles Lakers in June 2024.[^624] In March 2025, a second season was announced with [Steve Nash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Nash "Steve Nash") replacing Redick as James' co-host.[^625]

### Investments and other ventures

In 2012, James, Maverick Carter and [Paul Wachter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Wachter "Paul Wachter") made an investment of less than $1 million in the [Pasadena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pasadena,_California "Pasadena, California") -based [fast casual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fast_casual_restaurant "Fast casual restaurant") chain [Blaze Pizza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blaze_Pizza "Blaze Pizza"); their investment had grown to $25 million by 2017.[^626] James later became a spokesman for the company and began appearing in advertisements after ending his contract with McDonald's.[^626]

James partnered with [Arnold Schwarzenegger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arnold_Schwarzenegger "Arnold Schwarzenegger") in 2018 to found Ladder, a company that developed nutritional supplements to help athletes with severe cramps, which James experienced during the [2014 NBA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_NBA_Finals "2014 NBA Finals").[^627]

During the 2019 offseason, James filed for a trademark on the term " [Taco Tuesday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taco_Tuesday "Taco Tuesday") " for use in downloadable audio and visual works, podcasts, social media, online marketing, and entertainment services.[^628] James had used the term on [Instagram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instagram "Instagram") to refer to his family's taco dinners. The request was denied by the [United States Patent and Trademark Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Patent_and_Trademark_Office "United States Patent and Trademark Office"), stating that "Taco Tuesday" was "a commonplace term, message or expression widely used by a variety of sources that merely conveys an ordinary, familiar, well-recognized concept or sentiment." [^629] In November 2020, James became an [angel investor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angel_investor "Angel investor") of the [tequila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tequila "Tequila") and [mezcal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mezcal "Mezcal") company [Lobos 1707](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lobos_1707 "Lobos 1707").[^630]

After personal frustration with comments on the [Black Lives Matter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Lives_Matter "Black Lives Matter") movement made by [Republican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Party_\(United_States\) "Republican Party (United States)") U.S. senator [Kelly Loeffler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kelly_Loeffler "Kelly Loeffler"), who at the time was the owner of the [WNBA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women%27s_National_Basketball_Association "Women's National Basketball Association") 's [Atlanta Dream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlanta_Dream "Atlanta Dream"), James assisted Dream player [Renee Montgomery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renee_Montgomery "Renee Montgomery") in her successful bid to buy the team in March 2021.[^631] That same year, James joined [Fenway Sports Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fenway_Sports_Group "Fenway Sports Group") as a partner, making him a part-owner of the [Boston Red Sox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_Red_Sox "Boston Red Sox"), [New England Sports Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England_Sports_Network "New England Sports Network"), [RFK Racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RFK_Racing "RFK Racing"), and [Liverpool F.C](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liverpool_F.C. "Liverpool F.C.").; he had already owned a two-percent share in Liverpool F.C. individually.[^632] The investment made James and Carter the company's first black partners.[^632]

In June 2022, James said that once he finishes playing basketball, he would like to own an NBA team located in [Las Vegas, Nevada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Vegas,_Nevada "Las Vegas, Nevada"), either through [expansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expansion_of_the_National_Basketball_Association "Expansion of the National Basketball Association") or relocation.[^633] However, in March 2026 he said he was not interested in being part of an ownership group for a Las Vegas franchise.[^634] In August 2022, James and the rapper [Drake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drake_\(musician\) "Drake (musician)") became part owners of the Italian soccer club [A.C. Milan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A.C._Milan "A.C. Milan").[^635] James is also a part owner of the [Pittsburgh Penguins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pittsburgh_Penguins "Pittsburgh Penguins") of the [National Hockey League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Hockey_League "National Hockey League").[^636]

### Professional contracts

James' first agent was [Aaron Goodwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_Goodwin "Aaron Goodwin"). After leaving Goodwin in 2005, James formed the sports marketing company LRMR with his childhood friends Maverick Carter, Ernest "Randy" Mims and [Rich Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rich_Paul "Rich Paul"). LRMR handles James' marketing, which included the much-criticized television special *[The Decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Decision_\(TV_program\) "The Decision (TV program)")*.[^637] [^638] Following his departure from Goodwin, James had signed with agent [Leon Rose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leon_Rose "Leon Rose"), who joined [Creative Artists Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creative_Artists_Agency "Creative Artists Agency") (CAA) in 2007. Rose worked with fellow CAA agent Henry Thomas, who represented Dwyane Wade and Chris Bosh, to bring James to Miami in 2010. In 2012, James left CAA and became a client of Paul and the agency he founded, [Klutch Sports Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klutch_Sports_Group "Klutch Sports Group").[^639] [^640]

Throughout his career, James has taken a unique approach to his NBA contracts, usually opting to sign shorter-term deals in order to maximize his earnings potential and flexibility.[^48] For example, in 2006 James and the Cavaliers negotiated a three-year, $60 million contract extension instead of the four-year maximum as it allotted him the option of seeking a new contract worth more money as an unrestricted free agent following the 2010 season.[^643] This move allowed James, Dwyane Wade, and Chris Bosh to sign together with the Heat.[^644] During the [2011 NBA lockout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_NBA_lockout "2011 NBA lockout"), James received contract offers to play professional football from the [Dallas Cowboys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas_Cowboys "Dallas Cowboys") and [Seattle Seahawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle_Seahawks "Seattle Seahawks");[^645] he gave the offers serious consideration and even began training with them in mind.[^646] During his second stint in Cleveland, based on a negotiation strategy devised by NBA agent Mark Termini,[^647] James began re-signing [^648] [^649] a new contract each season to take advantage of higher salaries resulting from the NBA's rising salary cap.[^224] In 2016, he signed with the Cavaliers on a three-year contract,[^650] which made him the highest-paid player in the league for the first time in his career.[^651]

## Philanthropy

James has maintained close ties to his hometown of Akron, Ohio, which is also where his charity foundation, the LeBron James Family Foundation (LJFF), is based.[^652] Since 2005, the foundation has held an annual bike-a-thon to raise money for various causes.[^653] In 2015, James announced a partnership with the University of Akron to provide scholarships for up to 2,300 children beginning in 2021.[^654] In 2017, he received the [J. Walter Kennedy Citizenship Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._Walter_Kennedy_Citizenship_Award "J. Walter Kennedy Citizenship Award") from the NBA for his "outstanding service and dedication to the community." [^655]

In November 2017, the Akron School Board approved the [I Promise School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Promise_School "I Promise School"), a public elementary school created in a partnership with the LJFF to help struggling students stay in school.[^656] The school opened on July 30, 2018, with James reflecting that it was his most important professional accomplishment.[^657] Other initiatives undertaken by James in Akron include the I Promise Institute at the University of Akron, the I Promise Village housing complex, the House Three-Thirty community center and retail plaza, and the I Promise Health Quarters medical center.[^49]

James is also an active supporter of various non-profit organizations, including [After-School All-Stars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/After-School_All-Stars "After-School All-Stars"), [Boys & Girls Clubs of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boys_%26_Girls_Clubs_of_America "Boys & Girls Clubs of America") and [Children's Defense Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Children%27s_Defense_Fund "Children's Defense Fund").[^50] In 2016, he donated $2.5 million to the [Smithsonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smithsonian "Smithsonian") [National Museum of African American History and Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum_of_African_American_History_and_Culture "National Museum of African American History and Culture") to support an exhibit on [Muhammad Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Ali "Muhammad Ali").[^665]

## Social activism

James has taken public stances on various social issues—many of them controversial—and has mentioned a feeling of obligation to effect change using his status.[^666] These issues include the [War in Darfur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Darfur "War in Darfur"),[^667] the [killing of Trayvon Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Trayvon_Martin "Killing of Trayvon Martin"),[^668] [Donald Sterling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Sterling "Donald Sterling") 's racist comments in 2014,[^666] the [Michael Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shooting_of_Michael_Brown "Shooting of Michael Brown") verdict,[^669] the [death of Eric Garner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Eric_Garner "Death of Eric Garner"),[^670] the [U.S. national anthem kneeling protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._national_anthem_kneeling_protests "U.S. national anthem kneeling protests"),[^671] the [shooting of Breonna Taylor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shooting_of_Breonna_Taylor "Shooting of Breonna Taylor"),[^672] the [death of Ma'Khia Bryant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Ma%27Khia_Bryant "Death of Ma'Khia Bryant"),[^673] [Kyle Rittenhouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyle_Rittenhouse "Kyle Rittenhouse"),[^674] the [Houston Astros sign stealing scandal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houston_Astros_sign_stealing_scandal "Houston Astros sign stealing scandal") and the 2022 [U.S. Supreme Court ruling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dobbs_v._Jackson_Women%27s_Health_Organization "Dobbs v. Jackson Women's Health Organization") that overturned *[Roe v. Wade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roe_v._Wade "Roe v. Wade")*.[^51]

After a racial slur was painted on the gate of James' Brentwood home in 2017, he commented on the difficulty of being black in the United States. He said, "We got a long way to go for us as a society and for us as African Americans until we feel equal in America." [^678] [^679] In 2018, [Fox News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_News "Fox News") host [Laura Ingraham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Ingraham "Laura Ingraham") told James to "shut up and dribble" in response to his political statements.[^680] That same year, James used Ingraham's statement as the title for a [Showtime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Showtime_\(TV_network\) "Showtime (TV network)") documentary film series he produced, *Shut Up and Dribble*, which examines the role of athletes in politics and culture.[^681] [^682]

In October 2019, NBA executive [Daryl Morey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daryl_Morey "Daryl Morey") expressed support for [pro-democracy protesters in Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%932020_Hong_Kong_protests "2019–2020 Hong Kong protests"). After James said Morey was "misinformed", James was criticized by protesters in the movement, with many using racist language against him on social media.[^52] In February 2022, political commentator [Bill Maher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Maher "Bill Maher") called James hypocritical for not taking a critical stance towards the Chinese government's human rights abuses.[^688]

On August 27, 2020, James and his Lakers teammates, as well as the [Milwaukee Bucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milwaukee_Bucks "Milwaukee Bucks"), began boycotting the 2020 NBA playoffs to protest the [shooting of Jacob Blake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shooting_of_Jacob_Blake "Shooting of Jacob Blake").[^689] In response, senior White House advisor [Jared Kushner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jared_Kushner "Jared Kushner") said he planned to reach out to James regarding the boycott. Following a players' committee to discuss the boycott, James and others spoke with former president [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama "Barack Obama"), who reportedly advised them to continue playing and finish the NBA season.[^690]

In October 2023, following the [October 7th attacks in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_7_attacks "October 7 attacks"), James voiced his support for Israel on Instagram.[^691] His statement drew backlash for its omission of the Palestinians affected by the ensuing [Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war "Gaza war"), including from the Palestinian-American activist [Omar Suleiman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omar_Suleiman_\(imam\) "Omar Suleiman (imam)").[^53]

## Politics

In June 2008, James donated $20,000 to a committee in support of Barack Obama's [2008 U.S. presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Presidential_election_in_the_US "2008 Presidential election in the US") campaign.[^695] Later that year, James gathered almost 20,000 people at [Rocket Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocket_Arena "Rocket Arena") [^54] for a free [Jay-Z](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jay-Z "Jay-Z") concert and a viewing of Obama's 30-minute *[American Stories, American Solutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Stories,_American_Solutions "American Stories, American Solutions")* television advertisement.[^696] Eight years later, James campaigned for [Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton "Hillary Clinton") during the [2016 U.S. presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_U.S._presidential_election "2016 U.S. presidential election").[^697]

In 2017, James called U.S. President [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump") a "bum" after Trump withdrew a White House invitation to [Stephen Curry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Curry "Stephen Curry").[^698] The following year, James accused Trump of attempting to divide the country with sports, and declared that he would never sit across from Trump.[^55] In August 2020, James announced his support for the campaign of [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden") for the [2020 U.S. presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_U.S._presidential_election "2020 U.S. presidential election").[^702] James spoke in support of the [More Than a Vote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/More_Than_a_Vote "More Than a Vote") movement, encouraging African-Americans to vote and criticizing [voter suppression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voter_suppression_in_the_United_States "Voter suppression in the United States").[^703] [^704] In October 2024, James announced his support for [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris "Kamala Harris") in the [2024 U.S. presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_U.S._presidential_election "2024 U.S. presidential election").[^705]

## Personal life

James married his high school sweetheart, Savannah James ([née](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birth_name#Maiden_and_married_names "Birth name") Brinson; born August 27, 1986), on September 14, 2013, in San Diego, California.[^706] They have two sons, [Bronny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronny_James "Bronny James") and [Bryce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bryce_James "Bryce James"), and a daughter, Zhuri.[^707] [^708] Bronny was a standout high school basketball player, and was named as a [McDonald's All-American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonald%27s_All-American_Game "McDonald's All-American Game") in 2023, 20 years after James' selection.[^709]

James owns a 30,000 square foot (2,787 m <sup>2</sup>) mansion in Akron, Ohio, that he custom-built in 2003 at a cost of $2.1 million.[^710] During his stint with the Heat, James resided in [Coconut Grove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coconut_Grove "Coconut Grove"), where he bought a $9 million three-story mansion overlooking [Biscayne Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biscayne_Bay "Biscayne Bay").[^711] In November 2015, he bought a 9,350 square-foot (869 m <sup>2</sup>) East Coast-style mansion in [Brentwood, Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brentwood,_Los_Angeles "Brentwood, Los Angeles"), for $21 million.[^712] He purchased another home in Brentwood in December 2017 for $23 million.[^713] In September 2020, he purchased a third home in Los Angeles, this one in [Beverly Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beverly_Hills,_California "Beverly Hills, California"), for $36.75 million.[^714]

James' closest friends in the NBA are Carmelo Anthony, [Chris Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Paul "Chris Paul"), and his former Heat teammate Dwyane Wade. The four have been referred to as the ["banana boat crew"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banana_Boat_Team "Banana Boat Team"). During an excursion to the [Bahamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahamas "Bahamas"), James rescued Anthony when he was carried away from their boat by a current. Anthony credited James with saving his life.[^715]

In September 2023, new client names from the [Biogenesis scandal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biogenesis_scandal#Accusations_and_investigation "Biogenesis scandal") were released, including that of Ernest "Randy" Mims, a longtime friend and business manager of James, and David Alexander, a well-known trainer of prominent athletes who co-owned a cold-pressed juice and smoothie business with Savannah and also served as her personal trainer. The DEA determined that there "was never any indication that LeBron James did anything wrong." [^716] [^717]

According to James' business partner Maverick Carter, James was in the habit of spending $1.5 million per year on his body, as reported by *Men's Health* in 2021. This figure included paying for personal chefs and trainers as well as recovery therapies such as cryotherapy and hyperbaric chambers.[^718] In a March 2025 interview, James denied that he spends up to $1 million per year on his health.[^719] He told *[ESPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN "ESPN")* in 2018 that he drinks wine every night, [believing it to be good for his heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long-term_effects_of_alcohol#Cardiovascular_system "Long-term effects of alcohol").[^720] He also has a fondness for sweets, especially chocolate chip cookies.[^56] While experiencing [sciatica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sciatica "Sciatica") in late 2025, James removed desserts and alcohol from his diet for two months.[^721] [^723] In June 2009, he underwent surgery at the [Cleveland Clinic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cleveland_Clinic "Cleveland Clinic") to remove a benign [tumor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tumor "Tumor") in the [parotid gland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parotid_gland "Parotid gland") on the right side of his jaw.[^724] [^725]

[Fashion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fashion_industry "Fashion industry")

- First black man to appear on the cover of *[Vogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vogue_\(magazine\) "Vogue (magazine)")*, posing with [supermodel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supermodel "Supermodel") [Gisele Bündchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gisele_B%C3%BCndchen "Gisele Bündchen") on the April 2008 edition [^561]

State/Local

- 6× Cleveland Sports Awards Professional Athlete of the Year: 2004, 2005, 2006, 2009, 2015, 2016 [^751]
- South Main Street in downtown Akron renamed King James Way [^752]
- Six-story commemorative banner in downtown Akron [^752]
- Featured on *[Space Jam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Jam:_A_New_Legacy "Space Jam: A New Legacy")* inspired mural in Akron near his alma mater (St. Vincent St. Mary) and his I Promise school [^753]
- Featured on "Cleveland is the Reason" mural in [downtown Cleveland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Cleveland "Downtown Cleveland") (with other notable Cleveland area figures) [^754]
- Honorary lockers at [Ohio State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_State "Ohio State") 's football and basketball facilities [^755] [^756]
- LeBron James Home Court Museum in Akron, Ohio [^757]
